# Current work and handoff

Last consolidated: 2026-09-27. This is the only current-status and handoff document. Update rows in place; detailed history stays in Git. [Development](DEVELOPMENT.md) provides setup, checks, and historical lookup; [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md) provides accepted contracts.

## Additional 1.0.3 media work in progress (2026-09-27)

Scope: the three current requests under the ignored follow-up plan's 1.0.3 heading: selectable/cyclable/all-track video preview audio and per-track export retention (all by default); external SRT/VTT and embedded subtitle preview with wrapping, outlined UI-font text, synchronized timing, visibility toggling and signed 0.1-second timing shifts; and playback during native window movement/resizing. Subtitle editing is excluded; export preservation is a delegated design decision. Earlier ideas/questions remain held. The preceding completed audit below covers the previous plan, not these additions.

Native movement/resizing is implemented in 39a0b26: NativeCaption owns a nominal 10 ms HWND timer during the native modal move/size loop and requests ordinary winit redraw, including held movement without changed dimensions. No direct graphics callback or separate media clock is introduced. Exit/cancellation/destruction retire the timer; stale ticks, fullscreen and DPI transitions are guarded. Two hidden-native caption controls, three app resize controls, all-target Release Clippy and normal Release build passed in follow-up-move-size-timer-*. Actual mixed-DPI movement skipped on this all-96-DPI setup; physical gestures, compositor cadence and measured playback-FPS improvement are not certified.

Audio preview is implemented through 47a707e (preceded by ac9cd82 and 6bccde5): source-scoped Default/Track/All survives seeks, rate/device changes and retained-input reopening; View chooses tracks and Alt+A cycles them. Independent bounded workers align/resample/sum all tracks on one WASAPI clock. Timeline gain/tempo follows the sum, while export retention remains independent. Track-specific exact and overview waveforms reject obsolete deliveries; All uses the first source audio track waveform. Per-tab/source choices follow transfer, rename and Save As. Indexed AAC zero rewind targets the selected stream only for MOV/MP4/Matroska/WebM; the rejected broader rewind broke raw formats. Existing selected/timeline/resampling/PCM evidence is retained in follow-up-audio-track-*, follow-up-audio-mix3 and follow-up-audio-resample. The authorized long example was read only; its two AAC/48 kHz tracks were decoded at three positions, without an audible or cold-cache performance claim. The prior whole workspace baseline passed 1,718 controls; it predates the export changes below.

Audio export retention is implemented and verified: transactional checkboxes in Audio export options default to all and allow a silent video. Save and Save As keep each retained source-order track independently, including rates, offsets/gaps, metadata/disposition and per-track channel/normalization processing. Retained originals allow restoring excluded tracks in later saves. Selection is source/tab scoped and follows transfer/rename/Save As; job/completion snapshots prevent marking different options saved. Audio-only derivatives retain their preferred-source-track policy, explained beside the checkboxes. Alignment tolerates source timestamp quantization without deleting PCM and preserves decoder sample format so atempo arithmetic stays unchanged. Silent companion tracks stay silent while other tracks normalize; wholly silent nonempty loudness selections remain errors. Architecture and Development now describe the durable contract and relevant checks.

Targeted evidence: export10/11 pass 42 distinct affected controls and all-target Release Clippy. The five new runtime retention controls cover independent PCM for unequal rates/delayed starts, trim/timeline edits, subset/empty/invalid selections, rate/normalization/cancellation, coarse timestamp gaps, a single missing sample on an exact axis and AAC source-axis/PCM agreement. App controls cover English/Japanese checkboxes at three densities and compact action bounds, retained-original restoration after Save As/Save, cancel/stale rejection and hidden two-window transfer/rename. Keep export8/9 failure logs: they explain atempo format negotiation and the silent-companion loudness fix. These are automated/offscreen/hidden-native controls, not physical input or audible approval.

Broad verification is complete across follow-up-audio-retention-full and follow-up-audio-retention-final: 1,725 distinct controls passed (app 943, app examples 4, core 94, runtime 679, runtime integration 4 and runtime example 1); 143 opt-ins remain ignored. The first run passed app/core and 678 runtime controls but exposed a legacy metadata test comparing the first retained silent video track with the preferred-tone derivative. Only that test changed: it now checks both retained video streams and the preferred derivative. The final runtime/all-target run passed all 678 controls in its standalone feature set plus integration/examples; the additional workspace-enabled renderer CPU-accounting control had already passed in the first run. No product code changed between these runs. All-target Release Clippy and normal Release build pass. The final runner found one formatting-only assertion wrap; rustfmt and the subsequent format/diff checks pass. All owned runner/child handles are terminal. Retain the original failed receipt as diagnosis, not as the final outcome. No push, release, version bump or installed-app replacement was performed.

Subtitle reading foundation is implemented: source-scoped track metadata joins the initial playback probe, and a cancellable private FFmpeg reader returns bounded owned text or palette-run bitmap cues. The native subtitle wrapper has no Drop, so the new owner explicitly frees every decoded allocation, including error paths. External files preserve authored time zero; embedded tracks follow video's container origin. Core interval lookup handles overlaps, backward seeks, signed 0.1-second delay and edited-source mapping. SRT/VTT/ASS styling is flattened for future UI-font rendering; bitmap cues retain alpha, canvas and placement. Explicit clear events shorten display timeouts; an unclosed final bitmap stays valid through playback end rather than trusting estimated container duration. The reader foundation is committed as fad4e47; app integration is described below.

Foundation evidence: follow-up-subtitle-read7 passes nine controls (one core, six runtime subtitle controls, existing playback-probe and subtitle-only-media guards), with no skips; all-target Release Clippy and rustfmt pass. Generated files cover external SRT/VTT/ASS, two MKV text tracks with a five-second container offset, MP4 timed text, raw and embedded PGS, Unicode/overlap/exact millisecond timing, palette pixels/alpha/canvas, early clear/open-ended EOF, malformed/missing inputs, cancellation/size refusal and native reader cleanup. The embedded test also opens a paused WARP session and checks its subtitle catalog. Retain read1/2/3/5 failure logs as compile/lint diagnosis, not successful evidence. All owned runners/children are terminal. These are automated codec/core and offscreen session checks; subtitle appearance/physical input is unverified. Full workspace verification remains required after app integration.

Subtitle preview UI and reader lifecycle integration are implemented: View / Subtitles offers a native filtered picker, visibility, embedded/external selection and signed decimal delay. Active-video drops load captions without opening tabs; the drop guide describes that target. Default visibility is off; explicit load/selection enables it, and Show can choose an available source. A latest-task worker retains MediaInput through decoding and rejects stale serial/tab/source-generation deliveries. Subtitle readers participate in source-save/relocation cancellation and idle checks; loaded choices follow transfer/rename/Save As and leave edit history untouched. Painting uses source-mapped playback time, centered wrapped UI-font text with black outline, and cached active bitmap textures. Graphics recovery retires those textures. Unsupported TTML file input was removed from the filter after inspecting the pinned FFmpeg sources.

Integration evidence: follow-up-subtitle-ui4/ui5 pass 156 distinct controls (95 core and 61 app), with no skips, plus all-target Release Clippy and rustfmt. The app count includes six subtitle controls, all 52 menu-filter controls (one overlaps the subtitle filter), three drop-guide controls and one font audit covering 560 catalog characters. Text geometry covers English/Japanese at 100/125/200% and 1200x700, 360x200 and 180x100 viewports; menu controls cover source choice and actual -0.3-second keyboard entry. Other controls verify real external-file decoding, no new tab/edit/transport mutation, stale picker/result rejection, reader cancellation/drain and command scope. ui3 exposed an unassociated delay label; bind it to the numeric editor and inspect static-text accessibility values too. ui4 then exposed only the existing leaf-location model omitting the new dynamic submenu toggle; update that test model, and ui5 passes it plus drop regressions. Product code is unchanged between ui4 and ui5. Retain failed receipts as diagnosis. All owned runners/children are terminal. These are automated/offscreen checks, not physical input, a visible native picker or desktop appearance certification; they are not the final cross-crate/lifecycle audit.

Subtitle lifecycle and presentation follow-up: a whole-client control reproduced captions overlapping the fullscreen bottom bar. Reserve its 48-point transport band even while hidden, preserving a stable baseline across hover; normal content layout and native controls are unchanged. Generated two-track MKV checks now exercise actual session selection, pause/seeks, trim/delete/stretch mapping, signed delay, 1.75x preview and crossing a cue boundary on the running video clock. Two hidden windows share one D3D11 device; transfer/rename carry settings and decoded data. Save As/Save preserve those choices, and fresh embedded reads still use the retained original and its source timestamps. The original fixture bytes remain unchanged.

Evidence: follow-up-subtitle-lifecycle3/lifecycle4 pass 21 distinct affected app controls (eight subtitle controls plus 13 fullscreen controls with one overlap, and one explicitly enabled GPU control), with no remaining skips in that set. The generated PGS GPU control verifies translucent pixels and authored canvas placement at 100/125/200%, exact clear/delay/seek behavior, texture reuse while active and retirement/reactivation after clear or cache reset. All-target Release Clippy and rustfmt pass. Retain lifecycle1 as the fullscreen defect reproduction; lifecycle2 additionally exposed a test-only rename completion missing file-version identity, which correctly triggered the existing Save As guard. The fixture now supplies the same before/after identity as the production coordinator. lifecycle4 adds running-clock coverage to the already passing native save/transfer control; only test code changed after lifecycle3. Embedded text subtitle export is implemented: re-encoded MKV/MP4/MOV/WebM/3GP keeps readable source text tracks as plain text, independently of preview choice/visibility/delay. Project surviving trim/delete/stretch spans and global rate, flatten overlapping text for timed-text containers and round absolute boundaries to milliseconds. Preserve supported stream metadata/roles, omit empty tracks and use bounded staging sidecars as additional inputs to the same encode. A scoped watcher safely forwards cancellation to reader-owned native tokens; caption-free sources add no reader/watcher. External captions and bitmap tracks remain preview-only; unsupported output containers omit captions, and styling/attachments are not reproduced. The localized subtitle tooltip and Architecture state this delegated boundary.

Targeted evidence: follow-up-subtitle-export5 passes 22 controls (five runtime export, eight runtime reader/text, eight app subtitle/lifecycle/menu and one font audit), plus all-target Release Clippy and rustfmt. The existing GPU opt-in is not rerun here; its earlier lifecycle3 evidence is unchanged. Native outputs cover all five containers, two subtitle/two audio tracks, a five-second source offset, Unicode/literal/nested character references, overlaps, source-relative boundaries within 1 ms, trim/delete/stretch/global rate, empty surviving captions, bitmap neighbors and staging cleanup/pre-cancellation. Fresh app Save As/Save reads from retained originals still pass with caption-bearing outputs. SubRip needs one character-reference pass after native ASS conversion; WebVTT already decodes references and must not get a second pass. Keep export1/2/3/4 receipts as diagnosis: borrowed native interrupt callbacks were replaced with owned tokens, markup handling was corrected, MOV/3GP forced-role assertions were scoped to actual muxer support, and an unnecessary cast was removed. FFmpeg's raw UDTA retry warning is nonfatal; subtitle text/language/timing are verified. All owned focused runners/children are terminal.

Window placement follow-up: the next launch restores the last accepted visible window's normal position/size and maximized state. An all-window close selects its last focused owner; separate closes select the last one closed. Hidden destinations and cancelled dirty-close guards do not contribute. Only the initial process window restores the preference, so new/detached windows keep their existing positioning. Fullscreen retains its pre-entry normal placement; minimized state never starts hidden. Restore workspace bounds before surface creation, scale dimensions for the destination DPI and clamp the visible frame against the current monitor work area and minimum, allowing native invisible resize borders beyond the edges. Defer maximization until normal visibility and mark the first surface resize immediately, before queued size events. The configured logical minimum changes from 480x300 to 420x260; default startup remains 960x576. A bounded versioned preference is updated in memory on accepted closure and atomically published at host shutdown using a nonwaiting publication lock. Preserve future/invalid/unreadable records; forced termination does not flush. Tests never open the owner's placement file.

The optional outside-top resize request is investigated on the current frame. The native-reference diagnostic passes on all three available 96-DPI monitors: standard and custom visible top equals the HWND top, while left/right/bottom have seven invisible outside pixels. Negative top offsets return HTNOWHERE; the custom top remains one inside physical pixel. An extra outside region would require changing frame/input ownership rather than widening this HWND's existing hit test. That investigation retained native controls, corrected side borders and the one-pixel top. The later inside-gutter follow-up above changes only the inside hit height; it introduces no helper input window or frame redesign. Evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-window-placement-top-reference.log. Gallery batches now include dense full-width tiles with two-point gaps/no filename rows and a viewport-based local YYYY-MM-DD status; history delivery before source-presence scanning; and bounded ready-texture retention across scrolling, overlays and media-tab transitions. Returning Gallery frames reuse their original GPU textures while asynchronous source validation replaces changed/failed inputs. A completed Shell listing retires removed cards from that folder; Source Save and graphics recovery still invalidate the cache explicitly. Closed-strip preparation reserves ready pixels for the last visible Gallery viewport within the shared 64-texture budget. Detailed earlier checkpoints are fbee263, b79c919 and 997b354.

Preview scheduling now distinguishes viewport demand from offscreen prefetch, including closed-strip neighbors and cache-only tail work. Visible misses finish before speculative probes, while memory/disk hits still precede generation within each class. The app recomputes priority after removing ready textures and updates it when only the visible boundary changes. Missing visible work cancels an active path that moved offscreen; promoting that same path or consuming completed results reuses work. Existing generation guards, coalescing and the 64-key bound remain.

Gallery now advances beyond the 64-texture working set through one cache-only request at a time on the existing worker. Its newest-first candidate indices belong to the filtered-list revision and exclude BMP/unsupported paths once at projection rebuild. Completions do not upload/free UI textures or request a draw, individual source errors advance, and stale/cancelled results cannot revive a retired request. Foreground display demand preempts even a same-path cache-only request. Original-image/prefetch work, editing, queued navigation, export, pending folder order and overlays pause requests while retaining pixels; retained audio/video playback/loading permits visible cards while suppressing offscreen/tail speculation. The existing closed-strip tail now uses the same admission route.

Cache-only admission requires spare disk space before source access and rechecks exact encoded size under a nonblocking publication lock. It never prunes existing files or changes foreground memory entries/durations. Full/busy/unavailable storage stops the current pass without per-frame retry; changed history/filter or explicit cache invalidation starts a new pass. Foreground publication shares the cross-instance lock but returns generated pixels without waiting for persistence. A stable lock identity is excluded from pruning; failed partial writes remove their temporary file. These are per-window media-preparation gates, not system-wide scheduling or a promise to retain unlimited history.

Final verification: the full workspace/all-target Debug suite (app line-table override) passes 1,544 controls with zero failures and 125 explicit opt-ins ignored: app823, app examples4, core92, runtime620, integration4 and runtime example1. Audio/timeline context menus and Select/Deselect: audio rows expose Copy/Reveal/Rename/Move/Delete for the clicked listed file through the existing native preparation and host transaction, without selecting that track. The outside-press regression is reproduced: the whole timeline was incorrectly treated as a primary-click toggle opener, exempting it from early popup dismissal. Its opening-button release cannot dismiss it. Ignore only this owner's just-closed popup hit geometry when reopening; other layers and blocked views retain ownership. Nested-menu hit regions and ordinary menu-button toggling remain intact.

Image minification follow-up: pointer/Ctrl redraws reproduce a transient loss of high-quality reduction in the real hidden app at 125% density (mean channel error rises from 1.000 to 31.406). A smaller mixed UI/image fixture reproduces sustained hardware error 32.334, while WARP remains stable. Mesh geometry, sampling preferences and uploaded image data stay unchanged; direct readback verifies every retained mip against the independent area reference. Explicit SampleGrad and resource unbinding do not fix it. A dedicated mip-image shader now calculates LOD from UV gradients in source-texel units and uses SampleLevel for minification, preserving the ordinary shader for base-only UI/video/thumbnails and the selected smooth/nearest magnification. A half-level refinement reduces trilinear softness, clamped at the first reduced level once the footprint reaches two texels. The texture chain, source ownership, edit/export pixels, cache reservation, high-quality/fast settings and existing default remain unchanged. The owner's final default preference follows visual review.

The twelve vendored Release controls pass with five explicit opt-ins ignored, including bytecode equality for the new shader. The current dedicated shader's isolated 8706x5949 Release trial reports upload/generation completion of 33.86-39.13 ms and warm draw/event completion of 0.144-0.254 ms, with unchanged 276,202,864 logical mip texel bytes (base 207,167,976). Storage investigation: target contains approximately 290 GiB and the external development build root approximately 230 GiB. A historical Rust evaluation target accounts for approximately 177 GiB of the latter. The running app's LocalApplicationData tree is approximately 154 MiB: approximately 90 MiB of deliberately preserved update-failure evidence and 64 MiB of previews. Preview disk storage is bounded to 64 MiB by preview.rs; this is unrelated to Rust build output. Inventory evidence is target/tmp/maintenance-storage-inventory.json (several old target subdirectories denied enumeration, so its total is a lower bound). Two inactive incremental compiler caches total approximately 196 GiB and can be regenerated without changing application media-loading performance. Automatic approval review rejected their deletion as "blocked by policy"; neither cache was deleted. Keep distribution inputs, released assemblies, signing identity and recovery evidence.

The sibling .towavue-save directories are same-volume replacement staging and retained pre-save originals for Undo, not the deletion-retention path (which uses TEMP). Shared readers/Undo own the original and final drop schedules cleanup; exceptional publication failures deliberately preserve recovery bytes. Cross-volume relocation would add large copies. The owner chose hidden same-volume retention. Newly created save/retention staging directories now receive FILE_ATTRIBUTE_HIDDEN without changing their contents, volume, lifetime or recovery rules. Existing owner files/directories are not migrated.

Video export batch: re-encoded videos now request a keyframe every two output seconds across ordinary/timeline, low/high-depth and hardware-fallback routes. The default OpenH264 GOP was unbounded: the supplied 18.12 s sample and 204.167 s trim contain only one keyframe at zero (453 and 12,250 packets). The 29,527 s source and 1,776 s MKV instead show approximately 6 s/8.33 s spacing. Fresh paused hardware sessions measure 31-273 ms on the long MP4, 133-385 ms on the short sample, 176-2,608 ms on the MKV and 1,955-5,548 ms on the existing trim, excluding the zero-position samples. All reported zero CPU frame transfers. These are windowless command-to-decoded-frame timings with size/mtime preservation, not presented frames, physical input, cold-cache or background-UI contention measurements. Existing owner files are unchanged; old sparse-GOP files do not acquire new random-access points automatically.

OpenH264 now uses four slice workers for edited canvases with both extents at least 256 pixels; small/narrow outputs retain the old single-slice behavior. A six-run 1/4/8/8/4/1 comparison on the same five-second 1080p60 reference measures 8.97-9.49 s with one slice, 3.25-3.31 s with four and 3.33-3.36 s with eight. Four slices add approximately 0.6% bytes and change reference PSNR from 47.102 dB to 47.086-47.088 dB; geometry, 300 frames and five-second duration match. Keep the existing quantizers and audio path. An experimental audio-only input seek shortens a two-second late excerpt from 7.42 s to approximately 0.20 s, but differs from sequential PCM (about -79.7 dBFS RMS error with one-second preroll). It is not adopted. The first three-minute public export with periodic keyframes/single-slice encoding takes 332.08 s (35.72 s to nonzero progress), retains 10,800 frames/60 fps/180 s and 90 keys spaced exactly two seconds, and seeks at the sampled nonzero positions in 11-46 ms. The final four-slice public export takes 131.27 s (7.83 s to nonzero progress), produces 360,316,396 bytes versus 357,333,601, and seeks at the sampled nonzero positions in 10-47 ms. Both outputs have identical 10,800 frame timestamps, geometry/depth/duration, 90 periodic keys and compressed-audio SHA-256. The two full-length runs have different disk-cache histories; use the six-run short-clip comparison for the encoder speed claim, not a controlled cold-cache ratio.

Final affected checks pass 227 tests / zero failures / eight explicit opt-ins ignored, covering export formats/quality/precision, trim/rate/timeline frame and audio fidelity, cancellation/publication, retained originals and save recreation. Opt-in read-only seek and public midpoint-export diagnostics are documented in DEVELOPMENT. At that checkpoint, input-to-presentation investigation remained; the later app probes and requirement audit below close that investigation.

Native seek follow-up (2026-09-24): a new Release opt-in compares no auxiliary work, a real video-sheet worker, and video-sheet plus waveform workers, in forward/reverse workload order. Each case has an empty isolated app cache; it does not flush OS caches. All four owner references pass 18 seeks each with fresh target PTS, hardware frames, zero CPU frame transfers and unchanged source size/mtime. The short sample measures 118-386 ms across workloads, the long source 22-361 ms, and the old sparse-GOP trim 1.83-6.20 s. There is no consistent large background-worker penalty in this trial. MKV includes an initial 3.21 s outlier with no auxiliary work; the final no-work pass measures 110-300 ms. Warm-up/order bias prevents interpreting lower concurrent timings as an improvement. Worker-active flags and cancellation/completion results retain the actual overlap evidence; source loading, presentation and physical input are outside the timed interval.

The separate MKV demux control passes ten seeks across two fresh inputs: its first seek loads 214 native index entries and the warmed seek stage takes 0.14-0.26 ms. The existing hardware-stage control passes twelve direct/worker pairs at different positions: seek 0.20-228 ms, decoder initialization 0.07-0.63 ms, first decoded frame 7.54-26.67 ms and remaining preroll 3.24-156 ms. These trials have different file-cache histories/positions and do not identify the cause of the 3.21 s outlier. No speculative worker suspension or playback-product change is justified yet. Both new diagnostics are cfg(test), explicitly ignored, read-only on the selected source and documented in DEVELOPMENT; owned preview caches are drained and removed. Runtime all-target Release Clippy, fmt/diff and UTF-8/local-link checks pass. No full suite is rerun for test-only diagnostics: the preceding Shell checkpoint remains the production baseline. Logs: target/tmp/follow-up-contention-{mkv,short,long,oldtrim,demux,mkv-stages,clippy}.log; all 72 samples: follow-up-contention-results.json. The application probe below now supplies the missing command-to-submission evidence.


Application seek evidence: the explicit presentation-verification probe passes 60 seeks across the four owner references and the retained two-second-GOP export. It uses the real event loop, complete UI rendering and native Present in a visible nonactivating window, direct UI actions plus injected focused egui press/release events, and paused/playing modes. All samples submit a fresh target frame with hardware decode and zero CPU frame transfers; source size/mtime stay unchanged. Settings/cache are isolated per process and successful fixture roots are removed after exit. This is injected UI/native submission evidence, not physical input or compositor/scanout completion. The short sample submits in 127-410 ms, long source 29-227 ms, old sparse-GOP trim 1.78-5.87 s and new periodic-GOP export 13-56 ms; cache histories and clip contents differ, so these are per-file observations, not a controlled speed ratio.

AAC playback seeking: the earlier native app probe found two first MKV playing seeks waiting 38.57/15.13 s despite video readiness after 0.254/0.571 s. Exact coarse-timestamp AAC-LC reconstruction traversed the audio prefix while the first video frame waited on that clock. Under the owner's conditional approval after measuring differences, ordinary unedited, unranged 1x playback now uses the existing 250 ms compressed preroll for known AAC-LC frame lengths and container ticks no coarser than 1 ms. Save/export, retained intervals, edited timelines and legacy range/rate playback keep precise entry points. Other codecs/profiles/time bases are unchanged, and approximate starts cannot seed exact checkpoints.

The production-entry comparison reproduces 0 or eight frames of displacement at 48 kHz (0-0.166667 ms) on four authorized MKV positions. Three aligned one-second PCM windows are identical; the three-quarter window retains RMS error 0.000260218 (-71.69 dBFS, -35.67 dB relative to that signal), peak 0.004320024. Warm exact first-audio readiness is 387-1,229 ms versus 3.1-7.9 ms for playback, excluding input open. Earlier test-only cold/warm histories remain separate in cd71270 and target/tmp/follow-up-aac-alignment-results.json; they are not a controlled speed ratio. Generated 44.1/48 kHz PNS-disabled controls align exactly; PNS-enabled noise can retain aligned RMS error up to 0.2013. This establishes decoder noise-state differences, not general inaudibility or a conclusive explanation of the private reference residual. No physical listening assessment is claimed. No background exact PCM splice is added: the measured sample displacement does not justify a mid-playback discontinuity or extra full-prefix I/O. Precise decode remains available to editing/export.

Final native application probes complete 24 direct/injected-pointer seeks in isolated, visible nonactivating windows with actual WASAPI clocks (muted), D3D11VA and zero CPU frame transfers. MKV playing first submission is 104-321 ms; the short MP4 control is 134-397 ms. A protected first target frame can initially trail audio, so the probe now observes both clocks advancing for at least 100 ms after initial catch-up. That gate completes 309-533 ms after the MKV command and 354-627 ms after the MP4 command; the observed stable video-minus-audio ranges are -39.0..+4.3 ms and -40.4..+3.9 ms, respectively. This is native clock/submission evidence, not physical sound/scanout or cold-cache performance. The initial probe incorrectly applied a flat 40 ms cutoff to every held-frame observation, failing the 25 fps MP4 around -41.6 ms. The final gate accounts for one source frame plus the existing late tolerance and retains actual gap ranges; product clock/drop behavior is unchanged. Both references preserve size/mtime and write no source pixels. Probe folders remain under target/tmp; a combined launch/cleanup command was rejected by automatic review, and the successful launches omit cleanup.

Performance requirement audit (2026-09-26): the parent seek/export request is now covered by the retained four-reference seek investigation, actual app submission/clock probes, midpoint three-minute public export, periodic-keyframe/slice controls and owner-approved AAC playback change above. Rechecked retained export evidence: both outputs contain 10,800 matching frame timestamps and 90 keyframes with identical compressed-audio digests; the parallel run records 131.268 seconds total and 7.827 seconds to nonzero progress. Rechecked both final AAC app records: complete=true for all twelve samples per reference. These are existing measurements from their stated builds/cache conditions, not new timings for the current speed-editor build.

The conditional caching suggestion does not require a new whole-file cache. Playback already seeks and primes bounded target queues. Current video-sheet requests map edited positions back to source time, select the surrounding sixteen-sample sheet and supersede obsolete work for priority hover requests; they do not first generate sheets from the beginning. Exact export retains its precision-preserving audio entry, including prefix work where required. Existing sparse-GOP files still require decoding from their earlier keyframe; newly re-exported output receives the periodic keys. Do not silently rewrite owner media, infer a large background-worker penalty from the inconclusive contention trial, or add an exact-audio splice without evidence. Physical listening/input, compositor scanout, controlled cold-cache ratios and arbitrary files/devices remain unqualified. The performance row is complete with those documented limits; no additional product change or benchmark rerun was made for this audit.

View > Language persists English/Japanese for the next host startup. Existing, new and transferred windows retain the current host's captured display language; graphics recovery restores it. One bounded native-runtime writer publishes versioned language.conf under APPDATA/towavue through a synchronized temporary file and nonblocking publication lock. Missing settings default to English; unreadable/unknown/malformed content is preserved. Pending choices disable the language submenu across windows. Successful publication updates the shared checkmark and queues a native Restart now / Later prompt, waiting for modals and following a surviving owner if necessary. Later preserves the next-launch preference and current windows. Restart now uses a distinct purpose in the shared tokenized all-window save/hold preflight; update worker acknowledgements cannot commit it. Selecting the current display language again cancels the next-launch difference and remains informational. The original preference/menu controls are in 9c96a53. Guarded immediate restart is implemented. Busy operations refuse admission; Cancel, stale save continuations and changed approvals retain all windows and edits. Successful Save as publishes before closure, while an accepted Discard remains provisional until every window approves. After normal worker retirement, release the host and launch receiver before starting the same executable. A real hidden child process proves fresh launch election and reloading the saved Japanese preference; ordinary close and launch failure retain separate no-retry controls. The restart opens normally without restoring tabs. Relaunch failure reports native manual-start guidance. Custom all-tab exit guards now use the same explicit discard-all wording as native guards.

Language-restart verification: the workspace/all-target suite passes 1,655 controls with zero failures (app917, app examples4, core93, runtime636, integration4 and runtime example1); 41+1+93 explicit opt-ins are ignored, not passes. Final all-target Release Clippy, normal Release build, formatting, diff, UTF-8/line-ending and local-link checks pass. Earlier targeted checks include native label IDs, actual fresh-process election, durable language reload, successful/cancelled/late Save as, changed approval rejection and updater-event isolation. Logs: target/tmp/follow-up-language-restart-{results.json,app.log,runtime.log,clippy-initial.log}, follow-up-language-restart-final-{results.json,targeted.log,workspace.log}, and follow-up-language-restart-guards-{results.json,app.log,native.log,clippy.log,build.log}. Native Open/Save as, frame/audio export, rename/move, recycle/update/unsaved prompts and About receive the captured language as value data. Globs/extensions, default choices, response IDs, cancellation/overwrite behavior, accepted destination identity, About links, user text and external OS errors remain unchanged. Nested runtime/host errors still require their separate pass.

Recent checkpoints are 2e0efd3 (Gallery/search/context actions), 8c21c01 (export/update progress), ec10876 (status/timeline/window labels), ab9d3f6 (toolbar/status/fallback dialogs) and 0a6f479 (main operation/loading/guard notifications). Timeline selection-drag now has optimized offscreen CPU evidence. The feature-gated probe bypasses native-window creation and uses isolated configuration, actual draw_timeline, synthetic overview/refined amplitudes and a paused/advancing UI clock. Settled runner 6628 passed formatting, feature Clippy/build and 72 scenarios (144 rows, 51,840 measured frames): widths 640/1920/3840, native density 1/2, no/overview/refined waveform and both directions. It checks retained detail every frame, zero held-drag seeks/commits and exactly one release commit. Worst case medians across refined cases are 295.5 us UI and 87.6 us tessellation; maximum per-case p95 values are 366.2/136.9 us respectively. These are separate phase statistics, not a summed percentile. No/overview median UI stays <=14 us. This does not identify held-drag CPU geometry as a material stall; no product mesh-cache change is adopted. GPU/presentation, real media begin/release dispatch, editing and worker contention remain unmeasured. Evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-timeline-cpu-settled-{results.json,root.txt} and timeline-cpu-settled-summary.json; the owned root retains CSV and exact feature executable. Initial runner 49124 found use of a test-only constructor; fixed to the normal isolated-cache constructor. Runner 40408 produced exploratory data but high-density refinement invalidated after initial layout; it is superseded, not accepted as detail-path timing. Hidden runner 39176 also passed formatting, normal-feature Release workspace/all-target Clippy and the normal Release build (target/tmp/follow-up-timeline-cpu-normal-*). Final localization coverage/layout and remaining MKV work remain open.

Hidden application seek verification keeps its HWND invisible and listening gain zero, driving ordinary rendering at a bounded 60 Hz cadence. The native-paint-only attempt timed out before sample zero (exit 101); do not restore that hidden scheduling assumption. The subsequent six-process/72-seek ordinary baseline and its limits remain in target/tmp/follow-up-seek-hidden-cadence{,-repeat}-results.json and follow-up-seek-hidden-all-{data,summary}.json. The feature-only probe now applies real centered Crop, middle-half legacy Trim or timeline Keep edits, supports the actual timeline pointer geometry, maps submitted edited frames back to source PTS and asserts configured edit operations remain unchanged. Across 16 isolated MKV/remux processes, all 192 paused/playing command/pointer seeks, 96 advancing-clock checks, hardware decode and zero CPU frame transfers pass with unchanged source stamps. The first panel-closed Trim trials include 2.775/3.252 s submission outliers; later panel-open trials across all modes finish within 410 ms. Target positions and cache/order history differ, so this is not evidence that opening the panel or using Keep improves seeking. A further eight-process/96-seek stage run reports synchronous output retirement median/max 2.618/11.415 ms, video join 0.368/28.792 ms, audio join 0.004/18.697 ms, queued-output startup 0.038/0.070 ms and producer startup 0.025/0.055 ms. These stages do not include eventual native audio initialization or decoder readiness and do not explain the earlier multi-second waits. Evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-seek-edited-{app,panel}-results.json, follow-up-seek-edited-all-{data,summary}.json, follow-up-seek-stages-panel-results.json and seek-stage-summary.json.

Native held-selection follow-up: the same hidden application now injects ordinary forward/reverse selection drags in paused/playing modes, preserving the captured-CTI four-point magnet. Eight live-work processes cover no edit, Crop, Trim and Keep on both references; four additional Crop/Keep processes require real completed waveform refinement. All 48 gestures/5,760 held frames pass: no held-movement seek generation changes, no early selection commit, correct released endpoints, final native seek submission, unchanged configured edits/source stamps and zero CPU frame transfers. The live batch observes 480 overview-pending and 480 detail-pending held frames; the remaining 4,800 combined held frames retain real refinement. Pending flags establish outstanding work, not worker CPU utilization. Per-case held render medians are 0.662-0.968 ms; p95 reaches 15.272 ms and a single render reaches 79.856 ms. Press/release render calls reach 47.427/80.691 ms; final release readiness reaches 572.315 ms. Explicit-cadence render-completion intervals have 17.594-17.936 ms case medians and a 96.678 ms maximum. Refinement completion does not eliminate the isolated stalls. These are render-call/native submission observations, not GPU execution, compositor, normal desktop scheduling, physical-input or audible-quality proof. The following phase study attributes the spikes; do not add a mesh cache or cancel waveform workers without causal evidence. Video/waveform/audio improvement assessment and the owner's performance rows remain open.

Held-drag phase attribution: bounded scalar burst snapshots now divide actual hidden render calls into UI preparation, media draw, UI draw, platform output, caption clipping, native DXGI Present and post-present actions. Two four-process Crop/Keep batches on the MKV/remux sources pass all 32 gestures/3,840 held frames. The split-caption batch measures maxima of 1.271 ms preparation, 2.003 ms media, 1.213 ms UI and 0.009 ms caption clipping, versus 78.015 ms inside Present. Spikes concentrate shortly after selection-press seeks; this is correlation, not proof of a particular GPU/driver queue cause. Native controls, waveform tessellation and clip refresh are not supported optimization targets by this trace. Eight further processes alternate sync intervals 1 and 0 within the same executable: 32 gestures/3,840 held frames pass, but changing the interval does not consistently improve stalls. Interval-zero Present still reaches 72.218 ms; retain interval 1 in ordinary builds.

A final four-process DO_NOT_WAIT diagnostic passes 16 gestures/1,920 held frames, completing refused attempts normally so no unsuccessful submission advances app presentation accounting. Of those held-frame attempts, 1,916 return success and four return WAS_STILL_DRAWING. Successful attempts still take up to 41.776 ms; refused attempts take up to 14.672 ms before an additional blocking fallback of up to 34.773 ms. Merely changing flags therefore does not establish a nonblocking UI fix. All 20 phase/policy processes also pass their 240 initial command/pointer seeks and 120 advancing-clock checks; edits/source stamps, hidden/muted ownership and zero CPU frame transfers are retained. This is hidden API wall-time evidence, not GPU execution, driver-stack attribution, compositor timing, physical input or audible quality. No product presentation policy changes are adopted. The remaining performance work must assess native/driver synchronization around seek preroll and waveform/audio costs; async presentation would additionally require preserving successful-frame accounting, input dispatch, resource lifetime and retry scheduling.

Phase/policy evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-seek-drag-{phases,present}-{results,frames,data}.json, follow-up-seek-present-ab-{results,frames,data}.json and follow-up-seek-present-nonblocking-{results,frames,data}.json; each owned root contains its complete burst.csv and probe.log. Formatting, feature-enabled all-target Release Clippy and optimized app builds pass through follow-up-seek-present-nonblocking-build-*. The bounded publication/snapshot control passes once without skips in follow-up-seek-drag-phases-build-records.log. Snapshots are exported only after workers stop and refuse incomplete/overflowed records or an existing destination. Normal workspace/all-target Release Clippy, formatting and a fresh 25.27-second normal app build pass in follow-up-seek-present-final-normal-*. This checkpoint adds diagnostic code and instructions; the previous full ordinary-suite evidence remains applicable only to its recorded product checkpoint. No push, release, version bump or installed-app replacement.

Hardware-preroll attribution (2026-09-26): eight paused-only hardware/software Crop/Keep processes pass 48 seeks and 16 gestures/1,920 held frames with actual decoder-route counters, hidden/muted ownership, completed waveform refinement and source/edit checks. While fresh video is pending, hardware Present median/p95/max is 0.542/43.893/77.849 ms (78 frames), versus software 0.303/2.410/2.996 ms (790 frames); after hardware readiness it is 0.173/0.240/0.712 ms (882 frames). Software readiness is much slower. The preceding full software Crop/MKV run fails its unchanged clock-settling bound after ~2.9 s first submission and ~2.84 s initial AV separation; paused-only success does not supersede that failure. Four further hardware processes pass 24 paused seeks/eight gestures and trace packet calls: decoder construction median/max is 0.098/1.159 ms, while worst held Presents (40.769-49.326 ms across cases) overlap later send_packet calls at packets 39-72. This points to hardware preroll/native synchronization, not an established decoder-construction bottleneck. It does not identify a specific internal driver lock. Local FFmpeg already releases its own mutex before DecoderBeginFrame E_PENDING retry sleeps; do not reapply the old upstream mutex fix or disable multithread protection.

Bounded submission experiment: verification-only EVENT-query pacing every eight preroll packets keeps the same device, target frames and decode path. An eight-process paused A/B (0 then 8 per source/edit) passes 48 seeks/16 gestures: pending-video Present p95/max falls from 39.448/52.464 to 11.045/22.622 ms, while seek submission median rises from 189.856 to 207.633 ms. A second eight-process full comparison reverses the order (8 then 0), passes 96 seeks, 48 advancing-clock checks and 32 gestures/3,840 held frames; pending-video Present p95/max falls from 16.660/67.025 to 9.207/28.300 ms and held calls over 16 ms fall from 11 to 2. Full-comparison seek submission median/max is 188.446/313.171 ms unpaced and 201.370/329.139 ms paced. Improvement is not universal: Keep/MKV maximum is 16.599 ms unpaced versus 21.907 paced. All runs retain real refinement, exact target bounds, edits/source stamps, zero CPU transfers and completed successful submission accounting. This supports a pacing candidate, not a final product policy or visible smoothness claim. The current helper is diagnostic-only and deliberately fails on query errors/timeouts. Next: settle the batch/latency tradeoff and implement bounded cancellation/error/device-loss behavior before any production adoption, then run affected/full regressions and broader native reference controls. Waveform/audio assessment remains open.

Evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-seek-software-{ab,paused}-results.json (including the retained full software failure), follow-up-seek-software-paused-{frames,data}.json, follow-up-seek-hardware-steps-{results,detail,data}.json, follow-up-seek-preroll-{batch,playing}-{results,comparison,data}.json and owned probe.log/burst.csv files. Feature formatting, all-target Release Clippy and optimized build pass in follow-up-seek-preroll-batch-build2-*; the initial diagnostic build's unsized cancellation callback error is fixed and retained in build-*. Normal workspace/all-target Release Clippy, formatting and a fresh normal Release build also pass in follow-up-seek-preroll-normal-*. This diagnostic checkpoint does not rerun or replace the recorded full product-suite evidence. No product policy change, push, release, version bump or installed-app replacement.

Bounded hardware preroll pacing (2026-09-26, verified): a further same-binary eight/four-packet comparison passes 96 seeks, 48 advancing-clock checks and 32 gestures/3,840 held frames. Four packets lower pending-video Present p95/max from 8.548/24.376 to 1.224/11.167 ms, but increase seek submission median/max from 193.754/350.571 to 347.871/558.214 ms. Keep eight packets for the readiness/tail tradeoff. The product worker now owns an optional same-device EVENT query, stops pacing at the first target frame and abandons pacing on unsupported creation, query failure or a 100 ms polling deadline. Cancellation returns ConsumerClosed and drops the query; no unfinished event is reused. Driver calls themselves are not interruptible by this deadline. Exact target/EOF selection and ordinary decoder/device-loss paths remain in charge. The diagnostic environment can still select zero for A/B; its default is now the product's eight packets.

Targeted verification passes Release workspace/all-target Clippy, the polling cancellation/failure/false-result control, a native windowless generated-H.264 control and a normal Release build (follow-up-preroll-product-checks2-*). The native control compares eight ordinary/range/terminal target cases off/on, confirms actual queries/native frames, cancels after query submission three times and reuses the input for all 180 frames. This checks timestamps/counts/lifetime, not GPU pixel equality or actual device removal. An additional fixture audit found unhidden runtime console children; those test-only launches now use a shared CREATE_NO_WINDOW helper (the video-cost fixture uses the same flag directly). Product GUI launches and explicit visible opt-ins are unchanged. Full fresh workspace validation passes 1,706 tests with zero failures (141 explicit opt-ins ignored), normal/feature Release Clippy and both builds in follow-up-preroll-product-full2-*. The first final app process completes all 12 seeks, six clock checks and four drags but exits 1 when exporting an overflowed 65,536-record trace after 124.715 seconds; it is not a timeout, and source stamps remain unchanged. Retain follow-up-preroll-product-ab-results.json as a failed phase-measurement run, not a successful complete recording. Do not repeat the unaffected full product suite for this diagnostic capacity change. Initial failures were an unwrap lint in the new test and an unused fixture import, fixed with logs preserved. Final product-path native A/B passes below; broader reference/waveform/audio work remains open.

The pinned LAV codec-specific review now resolves the earlier context-reuse question: D3D11 Flush delegates to avcodec and drops its display queue; non-DVD H.264/MPEG-2 avcodec Flush explicitly reinitializes codec context. A retained manager is not evidence of an unchanged FFmpeg context. No upstream code is incorporated; source pointers and the unmodified avcodec blob are in target/tmp/follow-up-lav-review.md. The independent bounded-pacing implementation is verified; waveform/audio assessment is separate.

Final product-path A/B passes all eight hidden, muted Crop/Keep MKV/remux processes using the compiled default of eight packets versus an explicit zero override: 96 seeks, 48 advancing-clock checks, 32 gestures and 3,840 held frames. No cold-cache guarantee follows from these warm comparisons, and the earlier 124.715-second overflowed run remains a failed complete-recording attempt.

Edited-seek audio demux follow-up: 36 windowless/muted baseline samples separate first-video availability from audio-clock readiness. A generated two-audio-track MKV/MP4 control verifies fresh-route PCM/chunk/PTS equality, non-first best-stream selection, cancellation and reusable audio-to-video decoding. MP4 read counts and latency also fall. Counts include cached reads, not physical disk traffic. The unchanged 200 ms controls produce 9,600 frames for MKV and 9,568 for its MP4 remux; the cross-container difference remains an audio-audit observation, not a diagnosed regression. Full workspace/all-target verification passes 1,667 tests with zero failures (app923, app examples4, core93, runtime642, integration4, runtime example1); 41+1+95 explicit opt-ins remain ignored, not passes. Release all-target Clippy, normal Release build, formatting and diff/UTF-8/newline checks pass. Windowless/decode-only evidence does not prove GPU submission, audible synchronization or physical input. Automation remains hidden; no push, release, installed-app replacement or version bump.

Generated PNS on/off references pass 36 edit/rate/endpoint cases. Final owner controls pass 54 cases at one quarter, midpoint and three quarters across MKV/MP4: all counts, PTS, chunk bounds, source stamps and tempo-fallback PCM agree; MP4 PCM is identical in every case. Eligible MKV phase differs by eight samples (0.167 ms). Some cases retain decoder waveform residuals after alignment; maximum measured interior RMS is 0.000854 and peak is 0.030931, with abs(lag)+2 frames excluded only around fixed edit boundaries/endpoints. Raw errors and exclusion counts remain in the logs. Do not infer inaudibility from these measurements; no physical listening or background exact splice is performed.

Eight alternating original/candidate session processes pass 144 windowless/muted seeks. MKV playing audio median falls from 562.861 to 93.044 ms for a legacy range and 548.732 to 83.325 ms for the Keep timeline. Broader reference seek verification (2026-09-26): the existing short high-resolution MP4, new two-second-GOP export and original eight-hour MP4 pass 144 ordinary/cropped seeks and 72 advancing-clock checks across twelve hidden, muted processes using the same current diagnostic executable. All 72 paired first-source timestamps match between pacing disabled and the product default, with hardware decoding, zero CPU transfers and unchanged source size/mtime. Median submission times (disabled/default, ms) are short 284/293 ordinary and 284/291 cropped; new export 51/82 and 56/82; long source 234/212 and 195/259. Default maxima remain 110-477 ms across these six cases. These results extend the preceding MKV/remux verification; pacing has a readiness cost, and uncontrolled cache/desktop state prevents attributing every difference to it. They do not certify physical input, visible scanout or cold-cache performance. The old trim was intentionally deleted after the owner confirmed its distinct lag resolved. Evidence: target/tmp/follow-up-preroll-broad2-results.json and analyze-preroll-broad.py, with complete per-process probe logs; all twelve processes exited successfully without timeout.

Waveform/audio assessment (2026-09-26): a new explicit read-only Release probe runs both owner references through the native overview and exact detailed whole-source, middle-eight-second and 1.1x-retimed middle waveforms. All six instrumented detailed outputs match every one of the 1,024 columns from the public API and the exact plan/rate frame count; source size/mtime remain unchanged. MKV overview takes 1,978 ms; public whole/middle/retimed detail takes 1,429/658/660 ms. Corresponding envelope aggregation alone takes 58.764/0.279/0.233 ms. MP4 remux overview takes 851 ms; public detail takes 622/57/65 ms, with aggregation 57.041/0.248/0.234 ms. The MKV middle exact path requests about 5.0 GB, versus 17.4 MB for MP4 before the public format probe. These are warm, fixed-order process read counts, not physical disk traffic; no compiler overlaps the trials and other desktop work is uncontrolled. Whole-source frame counts follow each container's duration, not an assertion of cross-container PCM equality.

Decision: retain exact waveform/export and tempo-changing audio. The remaining middle-waveform cost is overwhelmingly outside envelope aggregation; optimizing that already bounded loop cannot recover the decode/demux time. Selected-audio demux filtering and eligible listening preroll are already adopted and verified above. The MPC-HC/LAV review does not establish a further sample-exact waveform shortcut or justify changing target accuracy, adding a whole-source PCM cache, importing queue sizes, or a background PCM splice. No additional product optimization is justified by this assessment; it does not claim optimal performance on every source or audible synchronization. The new read-only opt-in, Release runtime all-target Clippy and formatting pass in target/tmp/follow-up-waveform-reference2-*. An initial constant-assertion lint prevented measurement in follow-up-waveform-reference-* and was corrected before the successful run. This is test/documentation-only work after the existing 1,706-pass product suite; no fresh full suite, installed-app replacement, release, version bump or push.

The final requirement/evidence audit is recorded at the top of this section. No implementation work remains in the current 1.0.3 follow-up goal; physical validation and release preparation remain outside this completed automation scope.

## CI recovery verified (2026-09-21)

Both jobs pass in [run 35520198550](https://github.com/sheetau/towavue/actions/runs/35520198550) on e883219: installer lifecycle/recovery, workspace formatting/Clippy/tests, offscreen WARP, PNG row hooks, Rust notices/runtime materials, pinned MSYS2 inputs and read-only VC prerequisite checks. The workspace reports 1,510 harness passes and 123 ignored tests; explicit environment skips are not hardware or live-audio coverage. WARP reports eight passes/four ignored, and the targeted PNG row-hook test passes. No application behavior, production Setup requirement, published tag or release asset changed.

The original failures on 59cc25d were an expired BtbN daily download (404) and the generated Setup fixture retaining the Windows 11/non-Server gate on the Server 2022 runner. Both already failed before the Apache transition. The development-only FFmpeg pin now uses retained month-end tag autobuild-2026-08-31-13-27 with a verified upstream digest and release-specific cache; audited distribution inputs remain separate. Only the private generated Setup substitutes its supported-OS assumption. The Rust job allows 35 minutes for cold builds, attempts every workspace target and emits diagnostics immediately.

Previously blocked native tests exposed additional fixture assumptions. The audio-view and held-seek controls synchronously preflight WASAPI and report SKIP only for E_NOTFOUND; video-only seek cases still run. The frame-cache test separates same-byte/restored-mtime writes by 20 ms, matching the existing export control. Update fixtures canonicalize TEMP before reaching the helper's parent-identity refusal, including 8.3 aliases. The large Shell fixture establishes its own persisted name order before comparing three fresh snapshots; complete membership, exact path/kind order, canonical PIDL equivalence, distinct-item rejection and cancellation/recovery remain checked. A new folder's unconfigured first-use order is not used as the stable baseline.

Local evidence supplements CI: both audio-dependent app controls pass with a real endpoint and hardware/software video decode; affected cache/update controls pass, including a real short-path TEMP trial. The ordinary Shell suite passes 16 controls, with eight explicit opt-ins excluded. Local NSIS lifecycle/recovery, affected all-target Release Clippy and the notice/bootstrap/prerequisite scripts also pass. The retained hosted log is target/tmp/ci-repair-fifth.log; the local installer evidence is target/tmp/setup-update-lifecycle-26958ee9cd2940678b8006e161cdf9af. These automated/offscreen checks do not add Windows 11 physical-input, clean-machine prerequisite or published-update qualification.

## Apache-2.0 transition planned for 1.0.3 (2026-09-20)

The owner chose to keep the repository public and switch towavue's own code from MIT OR Apache-2.0 to Apache-2.0, with NOTICE, for the 1.0.3 distribution. Current Cargo metadata, README and About use Apache-2.0. NOTICE identifies the existing copyright owner (sheetau) and points to third-party materials. The original MIT text moved unchanged to third-party/towavue-legacy/; published releases, historical manifests and third-party grants are preserved.

Material collectors and Setup select the legacy license pair before 1.0.3 and LICENSE-APACHE/NOTICE from 1.0.3 onward. Current release assembly refuses a workspace-license/version mismatch. This is preparation only: the workspace version remains 1.0.1 until the 1.0.3 release preparation updates it and the lock notice digest. No Setup is published, no tag is moved and the owner installation/signing identity is unchanged. Exact 1.0.3 production assembly/qualification remains part of that release, following [RELEASING](RELEASING.md).

Verification: the material regression passes evaluation/1.0.2 legacy selection and 1.0.3/1.1.0 Apache selection, exact license/NOTICE copies, readable links, mismatched release refusal, missing/corrupt NOTICE and stale-MIT refusal without output creation. Evidence: target/tmp/application-license-b46a2ffe4883480da418d3e441dd198e. The real retained 1.0.1 package was rebuilt with the updated Setup builder, without installation: 95 binaries, 2,610 original mappings, 2,631 guide links, 2,709 explicit paths, 25 installer source files and archive/input refusal controls pass. Evidence: target/tmp/license-legacy-setup-build.log and local-setup-test-bca80db9f4a6488fbc3950f72c98428d. Occupied real registration probes and actual installation/prerequisite UI remain explicit skips. The existing About layout/dismissal test passes, including the rendered Cargo license text at supported densities; affected app all-target Release Clippy, fmt and diff checks pass. These are automated material, non-installing legacy package and offscreen UI checks, not exact 1.0.3 Setup or physical UI qualification.

## Published release and maintenance (2026-09-20)

The owner published [1.0.0](https://github.com/sheetau/towavue/releases/tag/v1.0.0) and [1.0.1](https://github.com/sheetau/towavue/releases/tag/v1.0.1), reports that 1.0.1 appears to work correctly, and requested documentation/repository cleanup only. Read-only GitHub inspection confirms both are public, with 1.0.1 latest. No new feature/UI work is inferred; held items below remain held. The current cleanup changes documentation and README images, removes obsolete local editing scratch, and leaves application code, repeatable tests, pinned material records, release outputs and recovery evidence intact.

### Release identities and retained inputs

| Release | Immutable tagged source | Local assembly and receipts |
|---|---|---|
| 1.0.0 | `14d040857e400d83444dd5c00416cc5c69935d51` | `target/release-1.0.0-14d0408` |
| 1.0.1 | `b5b64509ab4a6193e662e5f4771f301ee0c7f6dd` | `target/release-1.0.1-explorer` |

Both directories retain `RELEASE.json`, `DRAFT.json` and the five uploaded assets. DRAFT receipts describe upload-time state, not current visibility. Release IDs are 392305204 and 392318479 respectively. Each upload's names, sizes and server SHA-256 digests were verified against its signed receipt. Later maintenance commits do not retarget released tags or replace assets. The one-command draft workflow is in [RELEASING](RELEASING.md); publication remains an owner action for future releases.

Use the retained redistributable `ffmpeg-zvbi-verified/prefix`, including headers, MSVC import libraries and COMPLETE marker. Its 94 runtime files match the retained distribution inputs. The BtbN development runtime is excluded. Preserve the twelve native material kits and regenerate application/source/Rust notice bindings for each release. The existing Cargo target `target/launch-native-1.0.0` also contains the later 1.0.1 build; its directory name is not a version guarantee.

### Current implementation and verification

Production 1.0.1 registers 35 supported extensions per user for Open with/default-app selection and provides a static new-window verb under Windows 11's Show more options. It preserves UserChoice, extension defaults and thumbnail registrations; Windows supplies fallback file icons and available thumbnails. Ordinary file launches open fresh tabs in the last active surviving window; `--new-window --` uses a separate window in the same host/device. Folder launches retain their separate-window behavior. Registration ownership and reversible migration are documented in [LOCAL_SETUP](LOCAL_SETUP.md).

The final automated workspace baseline passes **1,510 tests, zero failures, 123 ignored** (app 805, app examples 4, core 92, runtime 604, decode 4, runtime example 1). The final live-window routing refinement passes five affected app controls. Three hidden native controls pass host/window/launch lifetime, ready-image startup and update startup/dirty cancellation. Workspace all-target Release Clippy, final app all-target Release Clippy, fmt and diff checks pass. Logs: `target/tmp/explorer-workspace.log`, `explorer-launch-final.log`, `explorer-native-verified-{0,1,2}.log` and `explorer-clippy*.stderr.log`.

Installer controls pass 35-extension coverage/quoting/collision/partial-retry/removal/preservation, registration/update planning, full transaction/recovery, native lifecycle and production NSIS progress/update/rollback. Production tests cover association rollback and all registration interruptions. Retained roots under `target/tmp`: `production-update-33f4f7f74cf14acc9d061d94d5992d63`, `update-transaction-490029b3842547d39858aacd24907217` and `setup-update-lifecycle-ac9fa9ef39694359add2fe4aa5a90f10`.

The final 1.0.1 package passes exact checks for 95 binaries, 2,610 original mappings, 2,631 guide links, 2,709 install/delete paths and 22 installer sources, plus refusal/mutation controls. Setup is 94,475,913 bytes, SHA-256 `c12d43882f3ef9b61fb1b2175e92f404973a27523894fc07846590b911205e57`; the source companion is 511,690,849 bytes. Packaged app is GUI subsystem 2/version 1.0.1, SHA-256 `55aa1ab3b26fa55354e50cefa8bb4a89efcda9fc44abfff8e3a920f95cb5256e`. Ordinary and explicit-new-window startup probes pass with Windows-only PATH/no FFMPEG_DIR and bounded error logging. These probes do not establish physical Explorer interaction. Evidence: `target/tmp/explorer-release-build.log` and `explorer-package-probe-{False,True}.json`.

### Update qualification and recovery context

The owner completed the repaired installed-update and manual UI/Finish guide, including dirty-edit cancellation, native notification, Install now progress, automatic restart and Install on next launch, then restored 1.0.0 before the public releases. The subsequent report accepts published 1.0.1 generally; it does not separately document a public HTTPS auto-update run. Owner-operated evidence is distinct from automated/hidden-window checks. Agent-operated visible UI was unavailable because Computer Use could not connect to its native pipe.

The earlier failed update returned Setup exit 5 with all 2,711 original files intact and a pending journal. Bound rollback cleared the pending registration without overwriting files. The exact historical child error was not captured; reopening during verification was consistent with the timing, not proven as the sole cause. The fix checks the active helper before transient registration fields, retains discovery errors, uses native TaskDialog/NSIS progress, waits for bounded transient readers and records caught apply errors beside the journal. The owner subsequently completed the repaired path. Consolidated evidence: `target/tmp/update-repair-results.json`; detailed diagnosis remains in Git at `a21b1d3`.

Preserve the archived failed generation under LocalApplicationData `towavue/updates-failed-20260920-26c824a4` and its bound recovery evidence. The local-only B at `ea389c67061c970fd7e56b55306112ee6148b385` (`target/launch-repair-local-1.0.1-ea389c6`) is a qualification fixture, not the public 1.0.1; never publish it. The production signing identity remains outside the repository. Its portable backup was completed by the owner; never regenerate the key or request the backup password.

### Accepted limits and documentation scope

Windows 11 x64 only; the initial EXE/Setup are deliberately unsigned, while update metadata uses the retained RSA-4096 identity. The owner accepted testing on this PC only. Clean-machine/VC-absent testing remains unavailable; synthetic prerequisites and recovery fixtures are not substitute evidence. The hardware H.264 playback control passed with zero CPU frame transfers; hardware encoding and VP9 Profile 2 hardware fixtures skipped, with software fallback passing. A verified public release listing does not by itself establish installed HTTPS update discovery/download.

The README now focuses on product strengths, installation and useful shortcuts, with four owner-supplied screenshots under `docs/images/`. Source/license audits and reusable regression infrastructure remain maintained. Earlier launch chronology, superseded trial outcomes and pre-publication visibility checks are recoverable from `a21b1d3:docs/STATUS.md`; retained release receipts and current evidence above are the next-work entry points.

## Scope and disposition

The previous concepts implementation goal completed on 2026-09-19. The owner then revised `follow-up-plan.txt` and opened a goal for its remaining work. That approved implementation is now complete on 2026-09-20, with the final Paste checkpoint and scope audit below; the prior concept audit remains historical evidence. The original concept is a draft; later owner decisions and accepted ARCHITECTURE contracts control conflicts. The previous follow-up revision had 29 checked implementation bullets, including nested requirements and two superseded proposals. Their marks alone were not treated as proof: the audit matched current source, named regression results, native/artifact evidence and the supplied visual references. `target/tmp/concept-completion-results.json` retains the exact plan texts/line numbers, input and source hashes, matching baseline regressions, current checks and artifact hashes. The tracked dispositions below and the subsystem ledger remain the portable handoff.

The pinned-egui picker tooltip hit-occlusion is still a known defect, explicitly deferred by the owner; do not mark the whole picker item complete or vendor a dependency patch. Other ideas beneath the plan's design-only heading remain unimplemented by instruction: filmstrip/gallery redesign, custom caption drawing, older folder-seek redesign ideas outside the newly approved precision gesture, sibling-folder navigation, uninterrupted selection during playback, HDR/tone mapping, buffered-cache bars and reference canvases. The new approval for ICC display and export controls supersedes their earlier exclusion, as recorded in New follow-up scope below. Grid editing UI is also explicitly deferred; text configuration exists. Design answers are retained in the plan. These exclusions do not remove their records or authorize implementation.

Human appearance/physical-operation approval remains separate from implementation verification: retain parenthesized completion marks. The owner accepted side-border spacing, restored toolbar/native controls, frame-step performance and the explicit Equals zoom binding. Other physical comparisons have not been relabeled as passes. Broader codec/device/DPI permutations in older ledger rows describe evidence limits or future hardening, not newly inferred requirements to exhaust every combination. No publication, installed-app replacement or push is included.

### New follow-up scope (2026-09-19)

New explicit implementation approval supersedes the previous design-only status for export format selection, three video-quality presets and LUFS/true-peak normalization. Embedded-profile-aware display is conditionally approved: investigate speed first; if a meaningful cost is likely, stop that implementation and report IrfanView's behavior plus the recommendation. The owner subsequently added concrete implementation requests for four-speed precision seeking (three equal upward bands plus the slowest range above them, with drag status; disconnect but retain drag-up timeline-opening code) and editable untitled tabs from pasted clipboard images, adopting the saved file after Save or Export as. These supersede the older seek brainstorming exclusion for that precise scope. Other design-only ideas remain outside implementation scope.

Approved remaining implementation is complete: pasted-image Untitled tabs now adopt their first saved file, and file-backed Save as adopts the destination while retaining Undo. The optional ICC display remains explicitly held for future work; the conditional speed investigation and recommendation were delivered. Frame PNG and Audio-only outputs remain derivative, so completion-notice middle-click is retained. The current plan's ten approved implementation bullets, including nested requirements, have individual parenthesized completion marks. Design-only ideas and answered questions are not silently promoted into requirements. Parentheses continue to mean implementation verified but human appearance/physical-operation approval pending. The final audit records the exact current plan, dispositions, source and artifact hashes in `target/tmp/follow-up-completion-results.json`. No outstanding implementation is inferred from earlier historical checkpoints.

Save as checkpoint: the native picker captures the accepted destination identity; cancellable preparation and the shared host publication gate retain the exporting document's first immutable original independently of an overwritten destination's old bytes. Full-media Save as adopts only the exporting tab, preserves full Undo/Redo and view/transport, and marks the exact exported edit/options snapshot saved. Undo keeps the new path and makes it dirty. Later edits remain dirty; background completion preserves the foreground document. Original-source peers keep their paths; loaded destination peers keep their own original and become dirty against the replacement. Source Save and repeated Save as continue from the earliest original. All affected audio queues, including background destination peers, request fresh native Shell enumeration; adopted audio queues preserve repeat and use the new folder. Frame PNG/Audio-only remain derivative and keep completion-notice middle-click. The command label is Save as; its persisted ID and shortcut are unchanged.

Verification: the serial workspace baseline passes **1,469 tests / zero failures / 122 ignored opt-ins / one owner-deferred picker exclusion** (app785, examples4, core90, runtime585, runtime integration4/example1). After the app/core phase completed, final review added only the app audio-queue refresh refinement; runtime sources stayed unchanged through the remaining baseline run. All **20 final affected tests pass / zero failures / one ignored opt-in** on the final source, including actual background audio Save as, destination-folder membership, repeat preservation, Undo/Source Save sample equality and the hidden native video control. The video control checks adopted destination, original decoder input and paused transport across two hosted readers; it is not a native audio-playback test. Other host controls cover stale/colliding destinations, cancellation, independent loaded peers, later edits, repeated saves, same-path Save and injected native acceptance. Initial app failures were obsolete derivative/path-only-dialog/cached-output test assumptions; the three subsequent fixture migration errors are corrected. Final workspace all-target Release Clippy, Shell lifecycle feature check, fmt/diff/local links and normal alternate Release pass, with only the two expected output-name warnings. All 453 source/configuration hashes match. This is automated, owned native/filesystem/codec, injected-dialog and hidden-window evidence, not physical-input or visible-picker approval. Evidence: `target/tmp/save-as-app-results.json`; review: `target/release/start-save-as.cmd`. Runtime groundwork evidence remains in `target/tmp/save-as-runtime-results.json` and Git. The Save as plan item has its parenthesized implementation mark; the later Paste checkpoint completes pathless adoption. No installed-app replacement or push.

Pathless Paste checkpoint (2026-09-20): Ctrl+V / Paste image creates a new dirty Untitled image tab from clipboard pixels and a privately retained lossless PNG. `TabTarget::current_path` and retained image views explicitly represent absent file identity. UI/history/Shell never use the private backing path; internal workers resolve it only through the owning tab. File operations, folder navigation, reading mode and filmstrip are unavailable before first save. Free rotation, resize, crop/flip, image copy and Undo use owned pixels; an unchanged empty edit plan remains unsaved. Save opens Save as; successful host publication adopts the real destination while retaining the first original and full Undo. Tab/window transfer preserves pathlessness, edited pixels and view, and final close retires private storage asynchronously without creating a reopen-by-path entry. Text editors, IME, recording and modals retain Ctrl+V; existing custom keys/prefixes and explicit empty bindings stay exact.

Verification: the broad app baseline passes **789 tests / three failures / 37 ignored / one owner-deferred picker exclusion**. It exposed an overly broad missing-path predicate and two menu fixtures with the old Paste-free order. Final code checks the actual Untitled tab variant; the menu fixtures include the new entry. All **207 final affected app tests pass / zero failures / 11 ignored opt-ins**. The remaining workspace targets pass separately on final source: **core90, runtime585, runtime integration4/example1, app examples4**, with 84 runtime opt-ins and one app-example opt-in ignored. Counts from overlapping runs are not summed into a single all-green workspace invocation. Earlier focused failures were fixture assumptions about edit-reset zoom and synchronous private-file deletion; their final controls pass. Owned RGBA injection checks lossless original retention, dirty/Undo, Save as/Source Save and existing destination peers; payload transfer checks pixels/view/lifetime, and UI meshes check the pathless canvas at 100/125/200%. Existing hidden native image/video Save and transfer controls remain part of the app evidence; no system clipboard writer or physical/visible-picker approval is claimed. Saved-file Shell order follow-up: Save/Export completion, directory-watch changes and F5 now request a new native item enumeration, carrying the current live Explorer sort/group settings into a private non-persisting view. Successful publication workers and refresh requests notify the folder, but correctness does not wait for Explorer's asynchronous re-sort. Live-setting/view failures retain the saved-Shell-view fallback. In the authorized reference folder, actual export/Source Save/re-export of one temporary sibling derived from `phoenix.png` all place it first while preserving the existing 2,671-entry native sequence. The original bytes/mtime remain unchanged and the sibling is removed. An initial extra assertion incorrectly required raw subsecond filesystem-time ordering: the unchanged native baseline already contains 80 such inversions, so final verification preserves Shell's actual sequence instead. The serial workspace passes **1,454 tests / zero failures / 122 ignored opt-ins / one owner-deferred picker exclusion** (app780, app examples4, core90, runtime575, runtime integration4/example1).
